**THE HOLE IS CLOSED AT ITS SOURCE — D-113 IS TRUE, AND WITHOUT 13.1-05.** `checkout-retire-sweep`
(`TZ=Asia/Manila 4-59/5`, registered) probes every hold that lapsed inside the last 30 minutes and retires
its PayMongo session, so an open tab or an unscanned QR can no longer charge a booker for a slot FitOut
has already given away. Plans 01-03 reconcile the aftermath of money we should never have taken; this
removes the case. `src/lib/payments/retire-checkout.ts` is the ONE policy behind it, and 13.1-05's three
inline reclaim wirings will call the same function — they are an accelerant, never a second guarantee.

**IT REACHES THE CASE NOTHING ELSE DOES.** A lapsed `pending` instant-book hold on a slot nobody else wants
is flipped by NOTHING: `request-expiry.ts` selects only `requested`/`approved`, and the lazy read
predicates free the slot for *display* while the row sits `pending` and its session stays payable forever.
A plan that wired only the three enumerated reclaims would have satisfied the enumeration and left the
dominant case wide open.

**PROVEN AGAINST PAYMONGO, NOT AGAINST A CALL COUNT.** A real `sk_test_` session
(`cs_95208137133e72b0722056fc`) read **`active`** before the shipped sweep and **`expired`** after, over a
fresh request. Its red was watched too: with the expire call deleted, step (5) read **`active`** while
`retireOne` still resolved `"retired"` and no stub count moved anywhere.

**THE TRAP IS HELD FROM BOTH SIDES.** A session the provider reports `paid` is never sent to expire **at
all** (a call count of ZERO, behind a guard-the-guard proving the stub is reachable), and no `booking` row
is written by any file in this plan — the lapsed hold stays `pending` **on purpose**, because that is
precisely and only what 13.1-02's `queryUnconfirmedPaid` selects. Cancelling it would silently delete this
phase's own guarantee for a booker who paid at the last second.

⚠ **TWO MORE PLAN INSTRUCTIONS DID NOT SURVIVE MEASUREMENT** (the sixth and seventh this phase).
(1) Break #1's predicted double-red is unsatisfiable — the *identical* derived-cron contradiction 13.1-02
recorded; case (c3) was proven against the drift it actually detects. (2) The red_watch's
`expires_at IS NOT NULL` row is wrong on both counts: that clause cannot affect a LIVE hold, and removing
it alone reddens **nothing** because SQL three-valued logic already excludes NULL twice over. Six breaks
applied, four red, two recorded as findings. The redundant clause was KEPT, with the reason written down.

⚠ **D-111 verified against the REAL dev database:** the shipped predicate returns **0 rows**. Both evidence
fixtures re-read `pending` / NULL `payment_id` / session id intact after every gate. Unlike an epoch
constant, this bound is structural — `expires_at` is fixed while `now()` only grows, so a row that has left
the 30-minute window can never re-enter it.

⚠ **ONE PREDICTION IN THE PLAN WAS SIMPLY WRONG AND IS RECORDED AS SUCH.** The latch is not what makes the
probe fire once: React's double-invoke happens at MOUNT, where `slow` is false and the effect returns
early. Removing the latch reddened NOTHING. It is kept as defence in depth and the component says so.

⚠ **gsd-sdk's state verbs were again NOT used** (project memory + the 13.1-01 incident note below). STATE.md
was saved to the scratchpad first and every edit here was hand-applied and diffed.

**11-20 is closed: SHELL-04 holds in code, and the one thing it cannot prove is named rather than glossed.** Three `opengraph-image.tsx` where zero existed — a root card, a listing card (title, `{Space type} · {City}`, the all-in RATE, never a computed total), and a **CONSTANT** invite card. The invite image takes **no argument at all** rather than an ignored one, imports nothing but the token table and the shared renderer, and returns **byte-identical bytes** (sha256 `1603284e7087bd70`, 12,781 B) for a valid, an unknown, a cancelled-booking, a malformed and a whitespace token against a production `next start`. The reasoning that made that the right design is in the file: the PAGE may differ between a live invite and an inactive one (showing the event is the product) and 08-06's property — unknown = revoked = voided = cancelled = malformed, one indistinguishable state — is preserved exactly, with all three inactive causes emitting the identical title and description; the IMAGE is a **new** unauthenticated endpoint that chat services, scanners and spam filters fetch automatically, so it may not vary at all. **THREE Next-16 landmines, measured, two of them walked into by the plan's own instructions.** (1) The DOCUMENTED font load — `fetch(new URL("./X.ttf", import.meta.url))` — does not work on the Node runtime: `new URL` resolves to `file://`, undici does not implement that scheme, and it rejects with `fetch failed`. Without the fallback rung already written, every card in the app would have shipped in `next/og`'s bundled face at weight 400, silently, at a 200. Fixed with `fs.readFile(fileURLToPath(url))`, keeping the `new URL` so Turbopack still emits and hashes the asset; verified in dev **and** against `next start`, where the production log carries **zero** `[og]` fallback lines. (2) **Declaring `openGraph` at all DELETES the file-convention `og:image`** and downgrades `twitter:card` from `summary_large_image` to `summary` — measured both ways on one route with nothing else changed, so the edit that adds tags is the edit that removes the card and it looks *more* complete afterwards. (3) The invite card is served at `/invite/<token>/opengraph-image-ikagei`; the unsuffixed path is a **404**. `getMetadataRouteSuffix` appends a 6-char djb2 hash of the parent path whenever any parent segment is a route group — so plan 11-10's `(public)` is what puts it there, and the plan's own acceptance criterion spells an unsatisfiable URL. **`new ImageResponse(...)` cannot fail safely by itself** — it extends `Response` and runs satori inside the body stream's `start()`, with the status line already committed, so a `try`/`catch` around it catches nothing; `renderOgCard` awaits `.arrayBuffer()` and returns fresh bytes. Three rungs, **all three watched firing**: a runtime font-read failure → 200 / 25,149 B / 1200×630 legible on both routes, and a forced rung-2 failure → 200 / 70 B / a real 1×1 PNG. The hole is stated too: a *syntax error* in the module is still a 500 (found by accident — a `**/` inside a block comment closed it early), and only `npm run build` catches that. **The conversion also fixed a shipped bug**: the invite title was hand-written before `layout.tsx:107` declared `template: "%s · FitOut"` and rendered `You're invited · FitOut · FitOut` on the app's only fully public link-shared page. `tests/design/og-routes.test.ts` carries AC#12/#13/#14 over the AST with an **allow-list** rather than the prescribed denylist (a denylist over `@/lib/db` is walked around by `../../../lib/db`), content closure stated forward plus a count, and **six watched reds** — including (d) vacuity, which turns 6 of 12 red with the guard first, and (e1)/(e2) paired because (e1) alone stays green **on purpose** and would have been another vacuous probe. Two pinned inventories moved, both watched red first: `STICKY_INVENTORY` 12→13 (**permanent**, unlike row-card's) and `DISPLAY_INVENTORY`'s two path keys renamed. `measurements.ts` gained its 8th and 9th constants because `auth-slot-skeleton.tsx` matches `patterns/*skeleton*.tsx` and AC#16 bans the literals the UI-SPEC prescribes for it. <details><summary>Previous status (plan 11-09, superseded 2026-08-13)</summary>

**11-09 is closed: three prop-complete patterns whose contracts are TYPES rather than conventions, and the gate over the absence the overlay decision created.** `patterns/empty-state.tsx` collapses the two drifted shells (two radii, two paddings, two title treatments, one with **no heading element at all**) onto shell A's on-ladder geometry plus shell B's heading, reconciled by `titleAs` — the rendered title can never be a `<p>`. Its `tone`/`icon` pair is a **discriminated union**, not two flat props: `tone="positive"` owns its `CheckCircle2` glyph and `icon?: never` makes the combination a compile error, because a flat required `icon` would have been silently ignored on exactly the tone STATE-04 asks for. Both hues are read from `STATUS_TONE_RECIPES` **by name**, so the panel is a fifth consumer of the status vocabulary rather than a fifth place that agrees with it by coincidence. `patterns/error-state.tsx` mitigates **T-11-ERRLEAK in the type**: five props and not one of them is `error`, `message` or `stack` — only an opaque `digest` — and `routeOut` is REQUIRED, so the bare-"Something went wrong"-with-one-button shape the UI-SPEC calls a failure of the contract **will not compile**. Both were watched: `TS2741: Property 'routeOut' is missing…` and `TS2322 … Type 'ForwardRefExoticComponent<…>' is not assignable to type 'undefined'`. The end-to-end `SENTINEL_LEAK_PROBE` proof is explicitly **not** claimed here — a prop type says what the component can be handed, not what five boundaries hand it, and plan `11-18` owns that. `patterns/responsive-dialog.tsx` is RESP-01's one overlay primitive, composed over `ui/dialog` through `className` alone: zero vendored files edited, zero packages, no registry block. **Its sheet presentation is spelled `max-sm:` throughout** rather than unprefixed-plus-`sm:`-restorations, so ZERO classes from the file apply at 640px and up — "the vendored centred dialog is byte-unchanged at `sm:` and up" is *literally* true instead of a claim about two lists staying in sync, which is the version a single mobile class added without its restoration silently breaks in every adopter at once. Verified in the compiled stylesheet: all twelve rules emit inside `@media (width < 40rem)` at offsets 85041–86322, after the base utilities they override and before the `sm:` block. **`--z-sheet`'s zero SURVIVED the arrival of the thing it was reserved for** — that is the fact worth writing down. The tree now has a mobile overlay and it renders at `--z-dialog` in *both* presentations, so the step still has zero call sites in **both signs**. `tests/design/sheet-absent.test.ts` is 18 assertions, watched red four ways: the block arriving (1/17, with the `FEATURES.md` F1 vs `ARCHITECTURE.md` §6.3 contradiction and its roadmap tiebreak **inside the failure message**, because whoever just ran `npx shadcn add sheet` is precisely the person who has not read the three documents), the token getting a home (1/17, naming file **and line**), the unit regressing (2/16 — both halves of AC#28 fire), and the vacuity probe (3/15) — where **both zero-call-site assertions and the empty-inventory assertion PASSED over a tree the scanner never opened**, which is the whole argument for the 50-file floor. **AC#28 itself was RED against the class it mandates:** the criterion's original substring ban `vh]` is contained in `dvh]`, so the only way to make it green was to ship the unit that clips the sheet's footer on iOS. Corrected to a bounded regex, and the correction is committed as a **runnable** assertion (`expect("max-h-[85dvh]".includes("vh]")).toBe(true)`) rather than a paragraph. Six deviations, two of them real bugs in this plan's own drafts: the prescribed `vh` probe went red because the comment explaining the hazard **quoted** it (the fifth instance of that shape this phase — fixed by `booking-row.tsx:112`'s name-it-descriptively precedent), and `aria-describedby={cond ? undefined : undefined}` would have unlinked **every** description the pattern ever renders, since JSX keeps a key whose value is `undefined` — caught by a jsdom render, now a conditional spread with 0 console warnings in both branches. Also added beyond the plan: a permanent `vaul` / `@use-gesture/react` ban (the dependency route into the same defect never creates a file called `sheet.tsx`, so the specified assertion was green while T-11-FOCUSTRAP was violable), and AC#26's presence half (the absence alone is satisfied by a tree with **no overlay at all**). Design gate **28 files / 514 tests** (was 27/496 — +1 file, +18 tests, exactly the new gate); `tsc` 0, build 0 ×3, lint 0 errors / 9 warnings — byte-identical to baseline. `git diff --stat HEAD~3 HEAD -- src/components/ui/ package.json components.json` empty; drizzle still `0025_audit_resolved_by.sql`. **No pinned count in `elevation-z.test.ts` moved** — these three components carry zero `z-` and zero `shadow-` utilities, verified over the AST — but two statements in it had become FALSE and were rewritten, because a stated reason that has quietly gone stale is worse than no reason. **STATE-02, STATE-04 and RESP-01 all stay Pending**: three patterns, zero adopters. `src/` now carries 10 of `selector-contract.ts`'s 17 declared ids. <details><summary>Previous status (plan 11-03, superseded 2026-08-13)</summary>

**11-03 (GATE-01's static half) is closed, and the plan prescribed a watched red that cannot fail.** `playwright.config.ts` now carries `updateSnapshots: "none"` **unconditionally** — no environment condition, so the guarantee holds on the author's laptop, which is exactly where an illegal baseline gets minted — plus a second project named exactly `visual` whose entry is **not constructed at all** off Linux (`--project=visual` errors with `Project(s) "visual" not found. Available projects: "chromium"`, after printing the named reason to stderr) and two disjoint `testMatch` globs, verified by dropping a probe spec into `e2e/visual/` and watching **neither** project collect it (total unchanged at 27 tests / 11 files). `@playwright/test` is pinned **exact at 1.60.0** so the container tag `v1.60.0-noble` cannot drift; `git diff` is exactly one line in `package.json` and one in `package-lock.json`. `.gitignore` gained its first `*.png` rules. The comment cites **RESEARCH Finding 3's measured behaviour, not D-135's wording**, because D-135 is wrong for 1.60.0: the default mode does not report green on the run that finds no baseline — it fails that run **non-retriably AND WRITES THE PNG**, so the next bare re-run is green off a baseline the failed run just minted. **THE HEADLINE FINDING IS THAT A PROBE LEAVING THE SUITE GREEN IS A FINDING, NOT A FORMALITY.** The plan told me to strip `#` comments before matching and to watch the comment-only fixture go red; the stripper was neutered and **the whole gate stayed GREEN, 4 passed**, because `hasRule` compares WHOLE LINES and a line beginning `#` can never *equal* a bare rule. Half 1 was already immune without any stripping. Skipping that probe — or rationalising its green — would have shipped a WATCHED RED section describing a mutation that never failed, which is 11-02's finding in a new disguise. Two fixes rather than one: a direct assertion on `ruleLines()` (Rule 2) makes the stripper's **real** job load-bearing — this `.gitignore` holds **50 non-blank lines, 17 prose, 33 rules**, so an unstripped floor of 20 would pass against a file gutted down to its own commentary — and a **fourth** probe (`hasRule` reduced to a substring check) covers the failure mode the fixture was actually written for. All four results are verbatim in the gate's header, **including the green one**. `tests/design/gitignore-baselines.test.ts` is 4 assertions, DB-free, inside `npm run build`, and is **the repo's first test to shell out to `git`**: half 2 asks `git ls-files` rather than walking the filesystem, because an untracked `*-win32.png` is harmless and expected (it is what a local `--update-snapshots` produces) and only a **committed** one is the defect — and because `.gitignore` is *advisory*, so `git add -f` bypasses every rule half 1 asserts. A failed `git` invocation is a **named assertion failure**, never a vacuous pass, and two control pathspecs prove the query shape still matches — one of them crossing a directory boundary, since a real baseline sits four levels deep. Probe (b) staged exactly such a file and **also corrected the gate's own remedy sentence**: `git rm --cached` does NOT delete the working-tree copy (measured — the PNG stayed on disk and `git status --ignored` then reported `!! e2e/visual/`, the harmless end state). Four deviations, all Rules 1-3: `e2e/helpers/theme.ts`'s amended paragraph claimed *"its absence from both is asserted by a grep"* — **no such grep has ever existed** (zero tests reference `playwright.config.ts`; every `screenshot` hit in `tests/` is prose), recorded in place rather than dropped; and the plan's NOT COVERED text names `11-21` for the two dynamic OBSERVED REDs when `11-22`'s own `must_haves` claim both. **GATE-01 stays Pending on purpose** — the static clause is closed, the dynamic pair belongs to `11-22`. <details><summary>Previous status (plan 11-02, superseded 2026-08-13)</summary>

**11-02 (GATE-04's regression-blocking half) is closed, and the plan's own probe design had a hole its author could not have seen without running it.** `src/lib/design/selector-contract.ts` declares all **17** structural hooks this milestone may render as a const tuple → derived union → **total `Record`** (D-31, superseding D-134's `SELECTOR-CONTRACT.md` name), every row carrying a mandatory `why` and an `owner` naming the plan that ships it. The compile gate was **watched**: deleting the `panel-card` row gave `tsc` exit 2 and `TS2741: Property '"panel-card"' is missing…`, recorded verbatim — and the *second* useful half of that output is that the required type prints the union **member by member** rather than as the alias, so the missing id is legible on both sides of the error. `tests/design/selector-contract.test.ts` is 5 assertions, DB-free, inside `npm run build`: the **D-32 floor** (`getByRole >= 92`, `getByLabel >= 30`, floors and never equalities, because an equality at 92/30 goes red on every legitimate new assertion Phases 12-19 write and the fix for that red is to bump the number — the rubber-stamp reflex arriving through the gate meant to prevent it), the **undeclared-id ban** (AST scan of all 128 `src/**/*.tsx`, every `data-testid` string literal must be in `SELECTOR_IDS`), and guard-the-guard over **both** trees. **THE HEADLINE FINDING IS THAT A GUARD-THE-GUARD PROBE CAN ITSELF BE VACUOUS, AND THE PLAN'S THREE PROBES WERE.** The plan prescribed breaking `E2E_DIR`; that gives **2 failed / 3 passed** because `0 >= 92` is also false, and a reader concludes the floor covers the vacuity case — exactly backwards. A **fourth** probe was added (Rule 2) breaking `SRC_DIR` instead: **1 failed / 4 passed**, and **the undeclared-id ban PASSED**, reporting a perfectly clean `[]` against a tree it never opened, indistinguishable from a real clean run and green forever. **An absence assertion cannot notice it was handed nothing** — only a positive control over its own scan can, which is why `tsxFiles.length >= 50` now sits beside `specFiles.length >= 10` and why `collectFiles()` returns `[]` rather than throwing (a throw is caught by whoever moved the directory; the realistic failure is a scan narrowed by a wrong glob, which never throws at all). All four REDs are verbatim in the gate's header, including the floor's, where **`.locator(` moved 23 → 24 in the same failure message that showed `getByRole` moving 92 → 91** — which is why the three ungated counts are printed inside the gated assertion rather than buried in a comment. **The measured counts are 92 / 30 / 63 / 23 and they are identical raw and comment-stripped today** — no spec quotes a query token in prose yet, so the shared `stripComments` changes nothing at HEAD and is there for the day a well-meant comment explaining a `getByRole` choice inflates the floor. Only `getByRole` and `getByLabel` are gated: a `getByText` floor would freeze copy this phase is about to rewrite, and a `.locator(` floor would be perverse — those 23 structural calls ARE the fragile selectors the phase exists to reduce. **`11-UI-SPEC.md` § GATE-04 says 22; the tree holds 23 and the measurement wins.** Two further plan-vs-tree corrections: the prescribed floor probe names a `getByRole("link", …)` in `public-listing.spec.ts` that does not exist (the file holds exactly two, at `:107` and `:112`; `:112` was used), and the phase's standing `ls drizzle/ | tail -1` check returns **`meta`**, not `0025_audit_resolved_by.sql`, because `ls` sorts the directory last — later plans should use `ls drizzle/*.sql | tail -1`. **GATE-04 stays Pending on purpose**: three plans claim it, and `11-05` (the (N+1)th-booking mutation proof) and `11-22` (the forward direction — "every declared id actually appears in `src/`") own the other two clauses. That hand-off is written into the gate's NOT COVERED block **and** made auditable by the `owner` column, because at wave 1 the forward assertion is vacuous against an empty set and from wave 4 it is red for every id whose owning plan has not run. Design gate **470/470** (was 465), **24 files (+1 exactly)**; `tsc` 0, lint 0 errors / 9 warnings — byte-identical to baseline; `git diff --stat package.json` empty (T-11-SC); drizzle still `0025_audit_resolved_by.sql`. **Zero source files were modified** — both artifacts are net-new, and `src/` still contains zero `data-testid`, which is what makes the ban's green a real 128-file result rather than an empty one. Next: 11-03.

<details><summary>Previous status (plan 11-01, superseded 2026-08-13)</summary>

**11-01 (GATE-05) is closed, and the plan's own `must_haves` contained a claim the tree cannot produce.** `import "server-only"` now guards eight money/availability computation modules, and `npm run build` hard-fails on any client graph that reaches them (D-34) — with **zero packages installed**, because Next aliases the specifier itself. Only after the first was repaired did RED #2 (4 errors) name the second. Adopting `server-only` on a multi-offender tree is a **fix-and-rebuild loop**, and a plan reading run #1 as complete ships the rest; what closes it is the emitted-chunk grep, a count over the whole output rather than a first-match trace. Both REDs are committed verbatim in the gate's header, with the sentence naming what did NOT fail — `slot-picker.tsx` and `request-row.tsx` are absent from every diagnostic, and no diagnostic mentions `slots.ts`/`read-model.ts`/`units.ts` at all, which is what makes the two splits correct rather than lucky. **The props-contract decision (RESEARCH Open Question 3) went to a TABLE, not a unit rate, on a measured ground:** `computeServiceFee` rounds ONCE over the whole space price, so `n × allIn(unit) ≠ allIn(n × unit)` — at the seeded ₱307.50 rate the multiply runs **ahead** of the frozen quote, bounded by n−1 centavos, which is D-75's forbidden direction. Both rail call sites' own comments call their figure *exact*; a multiply would have made those sentences false while every gate stayed green. That argument is now **executable** (`tests/booking/all-in-table.test.ts` case 2) rather than prose. `ListingCardData` **lost its five rate columns entirely** rather than keeping them unused — the inputs are structurally absent from the client, so the arithmetic cannot return without a deliberate props change. <details><summary>Previous status (plan 10-17 Tasks 1-2, superseded 2026-08-12)</summary>

Executing Phase 10 — plans 01-16 complete, **plan 17 paused at its human checkpoint**. **DS-13'S "FAILS THE BUILD" IS STRUCTURALLY MET FOR THE FIRST TIME, AND IT WAS WATCHED HAPPENING.** `package.json`'s `build` is now `npm run lint && npm run test:design && next build` — the load-bearing change, because Next 16 removed `next lint` and no longer runs ESLint during `next build` (L1) and `.github/workflows` does not exist (L2), so the npm script layer IS the gate boundary. `const LEAK_PROBE = "#ff0000";` injected at `src/components/ui/badge.tsx:18` — inside the vendored tree D-17 refuses to exempt — turned `npm run build` **red at exit 1**, `18:20  error  Raw design value "#ff0000" … fitout/no-raw-design-value`, `✖ 11 problems (1 error, 10 warnings)`, with `&&` correctly short-circuiting before the design gate and `next build`; `npm run test:design -- leak` then caught the same probe **independently of ESLint** at 1 failed / 17 passed; probe reverted, `npm run build` exit 0 in 72s. Both failing runs are pasted into `tests/design/leak.test.ts`'s own header, dated, naming what was injected and where. **Both gates read ONE list AND one SCOPE** — `eslint.config.mjs` imports `DESIGN_LEAK_PATTERNS` and spreads `LEAK_SCAN_GLOBS` into its `files:` key — with **zero new npm dependencies** (the plugin is inline; `git diff` on `package.json` shows exactly one changed line). **10-15's handoff is asserted three ways rather than honoured by omission**: `src/lib/design/**` is walked, is not scanned, and WOULD produce ≥40 violations if it were. D-17's positive control pins **30/30** vendored primitives visited. **THE HEADLINE FINDING IS THAT THE PLAN'S OWN DRIFT ALGORITHM WAS WRONG, and the tell was a finding neither of its two prescribed resolutions could close.** The naive cross product pairs `ui/calendar.tsx:221`'s `data-[range-middle=true]:text-foreground` with `data-[range-end=true]:bg-primary` — two mutually exclusive day states — at **~1.1:1**: adding the row would declare a 1.1:1 pairing legal, and "fix the component" means breaking a calendar that is not broken. So the CHECK was fixed, twice. **Narrowing A:** a foreground and a background pair only when their variant chains are identical or one is unconditional (`ui/dropdown-menu.tsx:76` does the same shape across a `not-` variant and the variant it negates). **Narrowing B:** `dark:` utilities are skipped because they cannot paint — `globals.css:32` scopes them to `&:is(.dark *)` and nothing activates `.dark` (provider writes `attribute="data-theme"` precisely to dodge that collision, `enableSystem` false, themes court/grove) — and because a skip is a hole unless its precondition is pinned, **all four mechanisms are asserted** and the header instructs that an activated `.dark` be answered with a THIRD THEME in the inventory, not a wider skip (watched red at 1 failed / 12 passed with `attribute="class"` substituted). What survived both narrowings is **one real undeclared pairing at two sites** — the inverted surface, `ui/tooltip.tsx:45` (`bg-foreground text-background`) and `listing/photo-uploader.tsx:278` (`bg-foreground/80`) — measured before being declared at **19.80/18.42** and **11.20/10.24** against a 4.5 bar, so **2 rows added** (29 → 31). Alias groups are **derived from token values, not typed**: the plan names two, the stylesheet has six. Drift watched red in BOTH directions — rows removed, and an undeclared pairing injected into a real component. **`npm run lint` measures 17s on this box, not the 85s the plan told me to record**; the accepted price of T-10-35 is ~29s of extra build, not 85. **D-9 DISPOSITIONED — accepted as debt** with the 119,835-byte figure reproduced by summing the emitted chunks (no rebuild): `@source not` would render `/dev/theme` unstyled, which is the very surface Task 3 is performed on, and a safelist is the second list D-16/D-18 exist to abolish; in context 10-12 took the stylesheet 134,132 → 119,079 and the route puts 1,103 back, so the phase still ships **−10.66%**. Design gate **375/375** (was 340), 20 files, DB-free; full DB suite **1197 passed / 4 skipped**, unchanged; `tsc` 0, build 0, lint **0 errors / 9 warnings** — byte-identical to the phase baseline with the new rule live. `npm run test:e2e` deliberately NOT re-run and the reason is stated: this plan changes no runtime code path (the only `src/` edit is two data rows in a module imported solely by `contrast.test.ts`), and D-6 item 1 has five byte-identical reproductions already. **DS-13 and DS-06 left Pending on purpose** — both are satisfied by Tasks 1-2, but Task 3 asks the developer to accept or reject D-11 (the accent deepening), and a rejection is a scope decision that could re-derive `--brand` and move every number DS-06 rests on. **NEXT: a human must open `/dev/theme` and rule on SC#3, D-11 and DS-04.** One correction for whoever reads Task 3's text: its `<what-built>` says "pinning the vendored 56" — the pinned number is **54**, and has been since 10-07 removed two dark-mode twins alongside two accessibility defects.

<details><summary>Previous status (plan 10-16, superseded 2026-08-12)</summary>

Executing Phase 10 — plans 01-16 complete. **THEME-04 IS CLOSED, and the phase's one real screen exists.** `/dev/theme` renders two themes SIDE BY SIDE IN NESTED `[data-theme]` subtrees — nine sections per pane, real booking components fed by identical fixtures, one `ThemePane` function called twice so a difference between the columns can only have come from the theme — and it returns **404 in a production build**, verified by `npm run build && npm start` (`/login` returned 200 in the same run). **A real Chromium confirms the whole contract travels through nesting, not just colour:** the grove pane paints brand `lab(48.18 -30.43 -6.04)` (teal) against court's `lab(49.17 65.89 41.55)` (coral), radius 28px against 14px, display 34px/700 against 28px/600, a radius ladder of 12–52px against 6–26px, and ink-tinted elevation at 2/16/-2px against court's pure black at 1/8/-1px. Zero duplicate DOM ids, nine sections in each pane, and no horizontal overflow at 320 / 375 / 1024px with court first. **THE HEADLINE FINDING IS NOT ABOUT THEMES AT ALL, AND IT WAS PREDICTED IN WRITING.** `type-scale.test.ts`'s own NOT COVERED header, written by 10-11, lists "it cannot see `cn()`/tailwind-merge precedence at a call site". This page is the first surface in the repo to use `text-heading`, `text-body` or `text-label`, and tailwind-merge classifies ALL FOUR named roles as text COLOURS — its font-size matcher rejects `display|heading|body|label`, its colour matcher accepts anything — so `cn("text-label", "text-muted-foreground")` returned **only the colour**. The role was being silently DELETED, with `tsc` green, lint green, the leak gate green and the source scan green (the class NAME is right; it just never survives to the DOM), and the element rendering at the INHERITED size. Same defect class as the slash-modifier trap, with a sharper edge. Fixed by registering the four under Tailwind's own `--text-*` namespace in `cn()`; +6 assertions (29 → 35) watched red at **5 failed / 30 passed** with the registration emptied, and the colour control stayed green — which is what makes it a size registration rather than a blanket `text-*` exemption. Nothing shipped moved: all 12 `sm:text-display` sites are plain strings that never reach a merge, full DB suite 1197 unchanged, 17 E2E specs unchanged. **The THEME-04 gate is 16 assertions across two files and the primary layer is the COMPILED stylesheet**, because that is the only artifact where a dropped `inline` is legible: three utilities must emit `var(--background)` / `var(--brand)` / `var(--radius)`, and the whole 130KB output must contain **zero** `var(--color-`. Watched red — `inline` deleted → **EXIT=1, 5 failed / 11 passed**, alias count **201** — and **the instructive half is what stayed GREEN**: every structural assertion (both theme selectors unlayered and source-ordered, both nested panes on the page) and **all four jsdom cases** passed against a build in which nested theming was completely dead. That is RESEARCH Pitfall 3 measured rather than described, and it is recorded in both test headers. The jsdom layer exists because 10-02's spike verdict reads `jsdom RESOLVES nested custom properties`, and it honours both of that verdict's conditions: raw `[data-theme]` blocks lifted VERBATIM by a CSS parse (never compiled output, which jsdom's `@layer` blindness would make vacuous) and `getPropertyValue` on the token (never a resolved colour, which jsdom never substitutes). **10-12's `shadow-sticky` zero moved here, in this plan's own commit, exactly as 10-12 said it would** — a one-entry inventory naming this route, and a compiled-output ABSENCE that becomes a presence resolving through `var(--elevation-sticky)`; the half of the zero that mattered (no PRODUCT surface uses the step) is still what the map says. Four smaller deviations, all Rule 1/3: a fourth file was required because a Server Component cannot hand `SlotPicker` its callback; the selected run is two real clicks ONE FRAME APART (same-tick clicks both read the empty state and re-anchor); `STICKY_INVENTORY` already existed in the same file for the z-index clause; and jsdom re-serialises the alpha slash in a multi-token custom property. Design gate **340/340** (was 318 at 10-15's close), DB-free, ~6.1s; full DB suite 1197 passed / 4 skipped; `tsc` 0, build 0, lint 0 errors / 9 warnings — all unchanged from baseline. `npm run test:e2e` 17 passed / 1 failed (D-6 item 1, a **fifth** byte-identical signal). **D-9 opened and measured rather than waved away:** the preview's utilities ship to production where the route 404s — 118,732 → 119,835 bytes on clean rebuilds of both sides, **+1,103 (+0.93%)** — with both removal mechanisms priced and rejected. Next: 10-17.

</details>

<details><summary>Previous status (plan 10-15, superseded 2026-08-12)</summary>

Executing Phase 10 — plans 01-15 complete. **DS-12 AND DS-14 ARE BOTH CLOSED, and neither was left Pending — the first two requirements this phase has been able to finish outright** (DS-02, DS-03 and DS-13 each had a clause claimed by a later plan; these do not). **The drift DS-12 exists to close was real and shipped:** `listing-map.tsx:22` carried `const BRAND_CORAL = "#E8484E"` annotated "matches the UI-SPEC accent", and it had not matched `--brand` since 10-03 re-derived that token for contrast — the stylesheet painted `#da2d34`, the map painted something else, and nothing in the repository could tell. `grep -Ec '#[0-9a-fA-F]{3,8}\b'` over that whole file now returns **0**, comments included. `scripts/generate-design-tokens.mjs` reads `globals.css` through the ONE shared parser and writes three committed artifacts — a 23-token-per-theme module with oklch + 8-bit hex, and two themed favicons — deterministically (`wrote 0/3` on a second run, porcelain empty). **It reproduced 10-03's derivation independently to the byte** (court `#da2d34`, grove `#13807c`), which is the cross-check a file compared against itself can never give; `--ring` landing on `#555555` matches the value `focus-recipe.test.ts`'s header had computed by hand. **D-18's "CI check" now exists in the only place it can** — there is no `.github/workflows`, so `tests/design/token-drift.test.ts` IS the regen-diff gate (7 assertions, byte equality with NO normalisation, watched red at **1 failed / 6 passed** on a one-character hex edit and green again after regenerating). **THE HEADLINE FINDING IS ONE NO LAYER OF THIS PHASE'S TESTING COULD SEE.** The plan's favicon shape — a `metadata.icons` entry plus a `useEffect` that re-points the link — passes `tsc`, lint, all 318 design assertions and `next build`, and puts a correct icon in the server HTML. Probed against a **production** build it ships **TWO** `rel="icon"` links whenever the theme is not court, **with the stale one LAST**: `["/icon-grove.svg", "/icon-court.svg"]`. Browsers commonly honour the last, so the tab shows court while the app renders grove — D-19 failing silently, through exactly the ambiguity the plan gives as its reason for deleting `favicon.ico`. The alternative shape (the component rendering a `<link>` for React to hoist) leaks the same way in the other direction, because hoisted metadata links are keyed by href so a change ADDS one and never removes the first. **The generalisable rule: a `<link>` React owns cannot be re-pointed at a different file.** All three shapes were built and measured; the icon now has ONE owner (no metadata entry, a plain element nothing else manages) — exactly one link in every state, still one after two navigations and a hard reload, `/favicon.ico` 404. The cost is stated rather than waved away: **252ms** of the browser's generic glyph on a cold load. **A consequence for 10-17:** `grep -c "icons" src/app/layout.tsx >= 1` now passes on the COMMENT that argues against the entry, not on the entry — recorded rather than quietly enjoyed. **Six files deleted**, each re-verified unreferenced immediately before removal; the lone `next.svg` hit is `ui/calendar.tsx:35`'s `rdp-button_next>svg`, a confirmed substring false positive. `.gitattributes` pins the three generated artifacts to `eol=lf` — `text=auto` plus `core.autocrlf` would hand a fresh Windows clone CRLF and turn the byte-comparison red for a reason that is not drift (git's own "LF will be replaced by CRLF" warning fired for the unpinned script and stayed silent for the three pinned files). `scaffold-residue.test.ts` extended 8 → **19** and watched red three ways. Design gate **318/318** (was 300), DB-free, ~5.0s; full DB suite 1197 passed / 4 skipped, unchanged; `tsc` 0, build 0, lint 0 errors / 9 warnings — all unchanged from baseline. `npm run test:e2e` 17 passed / 1 failed twice (D-6 item 1, a **fourth** byte-identical signal), and `public-listing.spec.ts` — the spec rendering the Leaflet map whose pin this plan rewires — passes 3/3 standalone. Next: 10-16.

</details>

<details><summary>Previous status (plan 10-14, superseded 2026-08-12)</summary>

Executing Phase 10 — plans 01-14 complete. **THEME-05 IS CLOSED, and DS-13's violations are at zero ahead of the gate that will enforce them.** Zero `dark:` occurrences survive under `src/app/**` or `src/components/**` outside the vendored tree — all 10 removed from the 5 files the plan named, in exactly the distribution it predicted — and the last 18 raw palette-class CODE occurrences across 6 files are rewritten onto semantic tokens, including the ONE vendored site (`ui/dialog.tsx`'s overlay) that D-17 refuses to exempt. **The headline finding is that the number this plan was told to pin had been wrong for seven plans, and the correction was already written down.** The plan says **56** in four places (`must_haves`, the Task 2 action's `toBe(56)`, its acceptance criteria, its `artifacts.contains`); the tree holds **54**. <details><summary>Previous status (plan 10-13, superseded 2026-08-12)</summary>

Executing Phase 10 — plans 01-13 complete. **DS-03 IS CLOSED — both clauses.** 10-12 landed the shadow half; this plan landed the z-index half, and every stacking decision in the app now reads from a four-step named scale instead of from two magic numbers. **20 code call sites migrated across 12 files** — 11 sticky (9 positive + 2 negative) and 9 dialog — with the 2 `z-0` on `calendar.tsx`'s range endpoints deliberately untouched, and **zero raw `z-10`, `-z-10` or `z-50` left anywhere in `src/`**, verified in the source AND in the emitted stylesheet. **The single most important finding is that following the plan verbatim would have shipped a visible regression its own gate could not catch.** The plan, `10-UI-SPEC.md` and every count downstream of them read `z-10 ×12`, because `grep -E '\bz-10\b'` **matches inside `-z-10`** — `-` is a word boundary. Two of those twelve are the "or" dividers on `/login` and `/signup`: a rule that must render BEHIND the label it crosses. Task 1's literal instruction ("replace all 5 `z-10` occurrences with `z-(--z-sticky)`") would have moved both from −10 to +10, in front of their own text, on the two highest-traffic unauthenticated pages in the app. They map to `-z-(--z-sticky)` instead, compiling to `calc(var(--z-sticky) * -1)` — value-preserving, still reading from the scale — and the gate counts the negative form as its OWN inventory so a future flattening fails a named assertion rather than passing a total. **The mapped total is 20, not the plan's 21, and the 21st is the twelfth and most dangerous form of this phase's grep-versus-comment collision**: `globals.css`'s CONSUMPTION FORM comment quotes `z-(--z-dialog)` verbatim while explaining why the bare form fails, so the raw grep lands on exactly the predicted number by a *different composition*. Every prior occurrence produced a WRONG number, which gets noticed; this one produced the RIGHT number for the wrong reason. (A 12th phantom site — `host-booking-row.tsx:121`'s comment quoting `relative z-10` — was rewritten descriptively, the convention `booking-row.tsx:111` had already established for exactly this.) **The consumption form was compiled and verified before a single edit**: `z-(--z-sticky)` → `z-index: var(--z-sticky)`, `-z-(--z-sticky)` → `calc(var(--z-sticky) * -1)`, and `z-dialog` / `z-sticky` → **nothing at all**, even force-safelisted. Tailwind v4 has no z-index namespace, so the readable-looking bare form is not an error, not a warning, and not a rule — it is silence, and that is now an assertion rather than a comment. **The `z-50` mapping is NOT value-preserving and that is deliberate**: `--z-dialog` is 30, so all nine former `z-50` layers moved DOWN together; `dialog.tsx`'s overlay and content were both 50 and are both 30, with DOM order deciding exactly as before, so the dialog still renders above its own overlay. Gate: `tests/design/elevation-z.test.ts` 31 → **53** assertions, **watched go red TWICE because the two failure modes have measurably different blast radii** — a `z-50` reinstated at `popover.tsx` gives **6 failed / 47 passed**, while DELETING the z-index from that same line gives **2 failed / 51 passed**, since every zero-violations assertion passes perfectly against a tree with no stacking at all. That second number is the whole argument for pinning exact counts on both sides, measured rather than asserted for the first time this phase. Two auto-fixes to `compile-css.ts`: its safelist sanitiser rejected every parenthesis and therefore the ONLY legal spelling of a named z layer, so it was widened — and because it is a security check, the break-out property it exists for is now **asserted** (six hostile shapes rejected) instead of documented only. Design gate 292/292 (was 270), DB-free, ~4.7s; full DB suite 1197 passed / 4 skipped, unchanged; build, `tsc` and lint all unchanged from baseline. `npm run test:e2e` 17 passed / 1 failed — **D-6 item 1, re-proven pre-existing** by checking all twelve source files out at `45ecf07` and reproducing an identical signal, because a stacking remap is the one change class that could genuinely put an overlay above its content. Next: 10-14.

</details>

<details><summary>Previous status (plan 10-12, superseded 2026-08-12)</summary>

Executing Phase 10 — plans 01-12 complete. **Every shadow in the app now maps to a named elevation step, and the stylesheet that ships contains only what the app uses.** The 14 call sites matched the plan exactly — 4 raised (`shadow-xs` ×1 + `shadow-sm` ×3), 5 overlay (`shadow-md` ×4 + `shadow-lg` ×1), 5 `shadow-none` deliberately untouched, across 10 files. This matters because Tailwind's default `shadow-md` compiles to a **literal**, not a `var()`: a theme block redeclaring `--shadow-md` does nothing, so all 14 surfaces were frozen against every theme and grove's pronounced elevation (D-02) could not reach them. `shadow-sticky` is left with **zero** call sites on purpose — reserved for bottom-anchored bars, none exist, and the zero is asserted so nobody invents a home for it; 10-16's `/dev/theme` ladder is its exerciser. **The plan's raw-grep criteria were ALL wrong against the tree, from one cause, and it is a new form of the phase's recurring collision**: the greps include `globals.css`, whose three `--shadow-*` TOKEN DECLARATIONS and explanatory comment are counted as call sites (raised 5 not 4, overlay 6 not 5, none 7 not 5, sticky 1 not 0). Eleven occurrences now, and the first where the artifact tripping the criterion was **the contract being tested**, not prose about it — fixed with a `(?<![\w-])` lookbehind. The `dark:` pin is **54**, not the plan's 56; D-2 already recorded the drop after 10-07, and it was proven unmoved rather than adjusted to fit. Gate: `tests/design/elevation-z.test.ts` 13 → **31** assertions, **watched go red at 5 failed / 26 passed** on a reinstated `shadow-md`, with the COUNT assertion firing — which is what makes a delete-instead-of-rename fail as loudly as a wrong name. **DEFERRED ITEM D-1 IS CLOSED**, the phase's oldest, nominated to this plan by 10-04 and 10-09 and owned by no later plan. One directive — `@import "tailwindcss" source("../")` — narrows Tailwind's content root from the REPOSITORY to `src/`. Measured on clean rebuilds: **134,132 → 119,079 bytes (−15,053, −11.2%), 119 dead selectors removed, 0 added, and 0 of the 119 used anywhere in `src/**/*.tsx`** (verified twice: a strict standalone-token scan, plus hand-checking the 8 highest-risk entries and confirming their real prefixed forms still emit). **The bytes are the least of it — removing the prose made two tests go red that had been green all phase, neither a regression.** (1) `@tailwindcss/postcss` caches its design system keyed on the INPUT FILE PATH, so every `compileGlobalsCssWith()` after the first silently returned the first one's output and `@source inline` was ignored; 10-04's "the default shadows are still literal" **control** had been passing because a markdown file said `shadow-md`. (2) Three DS-01 assertions read `.font-sans`/`.font-mono` rules no `src/` file uses — `font-sans` reaches the app via `@apply`, which inlines the declaration; DS-01 was never broken and is now asserted on the `html` rule and on `.font-heading`, which has two real call sites. **Compiled-output assertions are sound in this repo for the first time**, and DS-03 immediately takes the strongest form available: no default shadow rule in the emitted stylesheet, from any source — guarded by a control that the scan still reaches `src/`, since a root narrowed one level too far makes every absence assertion pass against an app shipping no CSS at all. Design gate 270/270 (was 251), DB-free, ~4.4s; full DB suite 1197 passed / 4 skipped, unchanged. DS-03 left **Pending** — its z-index clause is 10-13's declared part 2. Next: 10-13.

</details>

<details><summary>Previous status (plan 10-11, superseded 2026-08-12)</summary>

Executing Phase 10 — plans 01-11 complete. **DS-02 IS CLOSED — both clauses.** 10-04 declared the four-role token contract; 10-11 removed the 14 arbitrary pixel font sizes that would have made it a lie. The inventory matched the plan exactly for the first time this phase: 14 px sites across 10 files (8 route-level `sm:` Display titles, 4 component money figures, 2 sub-label numerals), plus the 4 vendored rem sites left untouched by the px-only decision. Every migrated site landed on a NAMED step — 12 × `sm:text-display`, 2 × `text-xs` — and the slash-modifier form appears nowhere, which matters because `text-display/tight` compiles to size + leading only and would have dropped the per-theme tracking and weight while looking migrated. The gate (`tests/design/type-scale.test.ts`, 29 assertions, was 17) was watched go red: an arbitrary pixel size reinstated at `src/app/page.tsx` produced 2 failed / 27 passed; reverted → 29 passed. The plan's own two numeric criteria were both wrong against the tree, in opposite directions, from the grep-versus-comment collision. The gate therefore strips comments before matching, and the stripper is line-oriented on purpose: a `/*…*/` regex treats `accept="image/*"` at `profile-form.tsx:109` as a comment opener and swallows the next 86 lines. There is a control on the control. Design gate 251/251 (was 239), DB-free, ~4.5s; full DB suite 1197 passed / 4 skipped.

</details>

<details><summary>Previous status (plan 10-10, superseded 2026-08-12)</summary>

Executing Phase 10 — plans 01-10 complete. **DS-10 IS CLOSED, and the status vocabulary is now a TYPE rather than a claim.** Two tone vocabularies shipped and neither was the design system's: `booking-status.ts` declared a 3-value union, `payout-ledger-status.ts` a 4-value one, and the value they shared was a filled green chip measuring 3.24:1. Both are now typed against ONE `StatusTone` — `neutral / positive / attention / soft-accent` — with `STATUS_TONE_RECIPES` as a TOTAL `Record` over it. **The compile gate was watched, not assumed:** a fifth tone without a recipe produced `TS2741: Property 'warning' is missing … but required in type 'Record<…>'`, `tsc` exit 2, then reverted, and the verbatim error is recorded in the test header. D-14 is enforced STRUCTURALLY: the three lifecycle tones share `bg-muted` + `text-foreground` and differ ONLY in a distinct icon hue, so a `positive` whose text carried the hue fails a test before any pixel is drawn. **FOUR filled-green chips were retired, not the three the plan's own line references implied** — confirmed, paid, payouts-enabled and the listing card's Live badge; the survivor count is pinned to the ONE glyph-only wizard step marker BY NAME (3.83/3.84 non-text, the single legal `--success-foreground` pairing). TWO of the four had NO ICON AT ALL and were therefore colour-only: the payouts-enabled banner (the plan flagged this) and the Live listing chip (the plan said "keep the badge's existing icon" — there was none). Both gained one. The gate (`tests/design/status-vocab.test.ts`, 13 assertions) was watched go RED on a deliberately reinstated `paid` fill — 3 failed / 10 passed, each failure naming the file, and the type layer correctly unmoved. Class matching is BOUNDARY-SAFE (`text-success(?![\w-])`) because a plain substring check reads `text-success-foreground` as `text-success` and would have reported the wizard as a positive call site. Design gate 239/239 (was 226), DB-free, ~5s; full DB suite 1197/1197 unchanged. Two shipped suites asserted the retired tone names and were rewritten to assert the MEANINGS instead (approved is not the paid signal; only confirmed is). Replacement classes verified to COMPILE from the production stylesheet, not merely to be present in source. Next: 10-11. NOTE — `soft-accent` is the one tone declared with no adopter WIRED: `spots-left-chip.tsx` already renders those exact three classes literally, but rewiring it moves a count `brand-recipe.test.ts` pins by name, so it is logged as D-7 rather than absorbed. The grep-versus-comment collision was AVOIDED for the first time by design in three separate places (the tone module's header, the wizard's exemption comment, and the two retyped view modules) — each names things descriptively instead of quoting the string its own criterion counts.

</details>

</details>

*16.1-01: ~23 min wall-clock, 3 tasks (all auto), 4 files created + 1 modified, 3 commits + 1 metadata.
The whole plan is one declaration and three gates: `upload-policy.ts` is now the only place in the repo that
spells the preset name, the byte ceiling, the 2048 edge cap, the six formats, the transformation or the photo
cap. ONE watched red, applied and reverted (the ceiling respelled as a round decimal million) and it produced
TWO independent failures — the VALUE assertion and a SPELLING assertion over comment-stripped source — which is
the point of pinning both: the value pin catches the number moving, the source pin catches a reader being
misled about which number it is. The finding worth carrying: the second failure's received value is a field of
newlines, because the module is mostly argument; a whole-file `toContain` would have been GREEN there, since the
docblock immediately above the declaration argues about the arithmetic at length. That is the falsely-green half
of `verify-workflows.mjs:24-32` demonstrated on this phase's own file, and it is why `tests/helpers/source-text.ts`
exists. TWO PLAN-INTERNAL CONTRADICTIONS auto-resolved (Rule 3 + Rule 2, both in the SUMMARY): the plan's action
text asked the module to quote the token its own acceptance criterion counts to zero.*

*15-14: ~35 min wall-clock, 3 tasks (all auto), 0 files created + 3 modified, 2 commits + 1 metadata.
Zero product surface and `src/` byte-identical: one test file made able to fail, two planning artifacts. FOUR mutations

at 131 passed), because that is the structural proof the token-layer gate cannot produce this failure. One

walking" and `Array.prototype.push` returns a length, so the arrow shorthand would have halted the `@apply`
walk after the first rule — caught by `tsc`, not by a test, because the callback's declared return type is
